# 06 — Prioritized Product Backlog

**Product:** Single-family rental operations platform (10–50 units, multi-entity)
**Status:** v1.0 — 2026-07-31
**Sources:** `00-master-prd.md` (single master PRD; §5 holds the epics and acceptance criteria), `07-decisions.md`

This is the single, strictly-ordered build backlog. Items are sequenced so that every item is buildable when reached (all dependencies appear earlier), foundation comes first, and the shortest path runs to the two golden paths: **(1) a tenant reports a leak from their phone, it reaches a vendor by text with photos, the vendor closes it with a photo and an invoice, and the cost lands on the property's books** (end of Milestone 2) and **(2) rent posts, autopay collects it, a delinquent lease accrues a jurisdiction-correct late fee, the reminder ladder fires, and the owner sees all of it on one rent roll** (end of Milestone 3). Numbering is global and continuous across milestones.

Sizes: **S** ≈ one short session, **M** ≈ one focused session, **L** ≈ 2–3 sessions or one long one, **XL** ≈ should be split during planning. Phase = the phase the feature belongs to per the PRD. A ✅ on the ID means the item is built and committed. A 🟡 means it is PARTLY built and the remainder has its own lettered row — used when a coherent, gated half shipped and the rest was blocked or deliberately deferred, so that a half-done item is never mistaken for a done one.

**Numbering.** R-numbers are global, continuous and permanent — never renumbered, never reused. The **#** column is positional. Rows added later carry a letter (`16a` = inserted between 16 and 17) so inserting an item does not renumber seventy rows below it. The `R-` prefix distinguishes this repo from the storage platform's `B-` series; the conventions are otherwise identical.

**Story IDs.** The PRD/Feature column points at `00-master-prd.md` §5 story IDs (PROP-, LEASE-, PAY-, MAINT-, COMM-, INSP-, DOC-, RPT-, NOTIF-, ROLE-, RISK-). Those IDs are the authoritative acceptance criteria — the row here is the work order, the PRD story is the definition of done.

---

## Milestone 2: Maintenance end-to-end (Golden Path 1)

Tenant reports it → triage → work order → vendor gets a text with photos → schedules with entry notice → closes with a photo → tenant verifies → cost lands on the property. This milestone forces the five hardest shared subsystems into existence: the notification engine, comms threading, file/photo evidence, magic-link identity, and the task queue in anger.

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 15 | ✅ R-015 | Property filing cabinet: deed/mortgage (ARM-adjustment and balloon-maturity alerts), insurance declarations with 45–60 day renewal alert, HOA docs incl. **rental caps**, warranties (roof/HVAC/water heater/appliances/home-warranty contracts) | PRD PROP-06 | M | R-008, R-012 | MVP |

"the one controlling this outlet may be in another room", disposal reset, thermostat battery, furnace switch, flapper, pilot) logging tried-or-declined before dispatch is allowed | PRD MAINT-01 | L | R-018, R-012, R-014 | MVP |
| 20 | ✅ R-020 | **Emergency intake path**: emergency categories (active flooding, sewage backup, gas smell, no heat in freezing temps, electrical burning/sparking, break-in, only toilet inoperable, CO alarm) show safety-first instructions (gas → call the gas company and 911 first), surface **the shutoff photo from R-014**, skip troubleshooting, and page on-call immediately regardless of hour | PRD MAINT-01, PROP-03 | M | R-019, R-016 | MVP |
| 21 | ✅ R-021 | SMS-to-ticket: a text to the business number creates a ticket and threads into it. Roughly half of real requests arrive this way; a portal-only intake measures adoption it created rather than demand that exists | PRD MAINT-01, COMM-01 | M | R-017, R-019 | MVP |
| 22 | ✅ R-022 | Staff-logged requests: phone-reported issues produce a structurally identical ticket (source: `phone_logged`) so the non-digital tenant is never a second-class record | PRD MAINT-01; D-10 | S | R-019 | MVP |
| 23 | ✅ R-023 | Triage queue **as a `Task` view**: suggested priority from category with override, merge duplicates, waiting-on-tenant state, first-response SLA timer with visual escalation, **habitability keyword auto-elevation** (mold, leak, no heat, sewage, infestation) into a tracked-response category with response-time logging | PRD MAINT-02, RISK-05 | M | R-019, R-011 | MVP |

Basements and rural properties have no signal; a flow that loses a tech's afternoon is a flow he stops using | PRD MAINT-06; D-8 | L | R-025 | ~~MVP~~ Phase 3 |
| 29 | ✅ R-029 | After-hours routing: on-call toggle (a two-person operation still needs one), escalation chain page → SMS → call → backup, emergency vendor list per trade, quiet hours respected everywhere except emergencies | PRD MAINT-12, NOTIF-05 | M | R-020, R-016 | MVP |
| 30 | ✅ R-030 | Verify & close (dependency on R-028 is soft — see D-17): tenant one-tap "was this resolved?" with rating, "no" reopens with a flag, **vendor reopen-rate captured from day one**; labor/materials/invoice attach to the property and to the work order — the WO → invoice → property-books chain is one chain with no re-keying, which is the specific place owners abandon software | PRD MAINT-07 | M | ~~R-028,~~ R-011 | MVP |
| 32 | ✅ R-032 | Work-order comms threading: tenant thread, vendor thread and staff notes hang off **one work order** with a single exportable timeline. Reconstructing an incident from three separate text histories six months later for an insurance adjuster is the problem this solves | PRD COMM-06 | M | R-017, R-025 | MVP |
| 32a | ✅ R-032a | **Somebody is subscribed to the vendor now.** Every vendor outcome raises work in the one queue (D-9), and the two that will not keep also notify. **The decline was the defect**: `sweepUnansweredDispatches` filters `vendorRespondedAt: null`, so a vendor who *ignores* us raises a re-dispatch prompt and one who *says no* raised nothing at all — the job returned to the unassigned queue silently and an urgent leak sat there overnight. Fixed at the event rather than by widening the sweep's query, because the sweep needs a timer to notice an absence and a decline is a present, timestamped fact that cannot afford an hour of latency. **A second silent hole found while building it:** a *proposed time* also sets `vendorRespondedAt`, so the sweep stepped over that too and nobody was ever asked to accept or counter the vendor's window. Accept and propose raise `workorder_schedule` (accepting is not scheduling — R-027 owns the window); a message raises `workorder_vendor_message` and notifies; an invoice raises `workorder_invoice_review` unless the ceiling already raised an approval, since two rows for one decision is worse than none. New `vendor_response` category so an operator can take the declines without the chatter | PRD MAINT-03, COMM-06; D-9 | S | R-032, R-025 | MVP |
| 32b | ✅ R-032b | **Two of this row's four claims were already fixed, and a third was wrong — verified before building.** *Already true:* the confirmed `scheduledStart`/`scheduledEnd` IS passed to the component and every timestamp on that page already used `utcToWallClock`; R-098's vendor-surface pass did both, and the row went stale. *Wrong:* `petWarning`/`entryPermission` are NOT "written nowhere" — all three ticket-creation paths persist them. **The real gap was that the vendor never saw them.** The PM's screen showed both; the person who actually opens the door was told neither, for every job since R-025. The pet warning now renders above the address as a bordered note — not a `<details>`, because a warning you have to expand is one that gets missed — and the entry answer is stated explicitly rather than inferred from silence, since "the tenant said no" and "nobody asked" send a vendor to different places. **And the completion photo is now actually required** (MAINT-06). D-17 asserted it was "already built into R-025's vendor upload" while justifying deferring R-028; it was not — the upload existed and nothing required it, so a job could reach WORK_COMPLETE with no evidence it was ever done, which is the state R-030 asks the tenant to confirm against and R-031 charges from. Gated on the server, not only in the page, and D-17 amended rather than left standing (D-41) | PRD MAINT-01, MAINT-03, MAINT-06; D-6, D-17 | S | R-025, R-019 | MVP |
| 32c | ✅ R-032c | **One-tap verification is now one link and one tap.** The SMS carried a portal URL behind `requireTenant`, which redirects to an **email-only** login with no return-to — so for the SMS-only tenant, the exact persona R-021 exists for, the message asking "was this fixed?" was a dead end, and for everyone else it was six steps. It now carries a single-purpose `TENANT_VERIFY` token: scoped to one work order, one tenant and one **round**, seven days, no session, no portal, no document, no money. **The answer is a POST, never a GET** — the row asked for a token "carrying the answer", and that is unsafe here because SMS clients and carrier link-safety scanners follow URLs, so a "yes" link would close jobs before the tenant read the message. The page presents both answers as equally prominent submit buttons, which is also what keeps the record honest. **Two bugs found building it:** `audit()` resolves its actor from a session and would have recorded the tenant's answer as `SYSTEM / anonymous` (fixed with `auditAsTenant`, attribution now an explicit argument at each door); and the recording logic was duplicated the moment a second doorway existed, so it was extracted to `verify-record.ts` before it could drift. **Left behind:** a "yes"/"no" texted straight back still reaches nobody — deliberately, see PROGRESS | PRD MAINT-07, NOTIF-03, COMM-02; D-10, D-16 | M | R-030, R-021 | MVP |
| 32d | ✅ R-032d | **Both halves, because they fix different failure modes.** The lifetime now tracks the JOB — three days for emergency and urgent work, which is attended within hours and where a long-lived credential that can reveal a gate code buys nothing; a fortnight for routine, which is booked out a week and where the link used to die before the vendor arrived. **And an expired link reissues itself** rather than saying "call the office", which was a phone call, somebody to answer it, and an invoice retyped by hand — the exact re-keying D-6 exists to prevent. That covers the month-end-invoice tail that no reasonable TTL reaches, without stretching every link in the product to serve it. **Safe because the new link goes to the vendor, not to whoever tapped**: a stale URL causes a text to the number on file and grants the clicker nothing. It refuses through `vendorLinkAccess()` — the same gate a live link passes — so reassigned, cancelled and closed jobs all decline, as does a link a PM deliberately revoked, a still-live token, and a forged one. One dead link sends one text however many times it is tapped | PRD MAINT-03; D-6, D-16 | S | R-025 | MVP |
| 32e | ✅ R-032e | **Decided: two numbers, two jobs, and the comment was the bug** (D-42). No behaviour changed — both functions were already correct and already used correctly. `jobCostCents()` is the books' number and takes the invoice; `actualTotalCents()` is the control's number and takes the higher of invoice and recorded parts. They diverge only when the parts exceed the invoice, which is why a doc-comment claiming they were "deliberately the same rule" survived until R-042's export had to pick. **Unifying either way breaks the other**: on the invoice, a $1,000 job billed at $600 stops tripping a $700 ceiling; on the maximum, a Schedule E return claims money never billed. A test now pins the divergence so a later unification fails loudly. **The spend tile says what it covers** — every job closed since the property was added, not a period or entity report — because `closedJobCostsForProperty` takes neither filter, and an unlabelled total reads like an annual figure somebody re-keys. R-081a still owns the real report | PRD MAINT-07, RPT-02; D-19, D-42 | S | R-030 | MVP |

> **Demo checkpoint 1 (Golden Path 1).** A seeded tenant reports a leak with a photo → the ticket auto-elevates on the habitability keyword → triage sets urgent → a work order dispatches to a seeded vendor by magic link → the vendor accepts a window (entry notice generated and logged) → uploads a completion photo and invoice → the tenant taps "resolved" → the cost appears on that property. No one logged into a vendor portal and no one re-typed an invoice.
>
> **Run it via the portal wizard, not SMS.** The checkpoint originally said "texts a photo", but R-021 deliberately deferred MMS (`NumMedia`/`MediaUrl0` are ignored; fetching media needs an outbound Twilio call that D-15 defers). The deferral stands — the checkpoint text was simply describing a path that was never built, and a demo that "fails" for a reason nobody wrote down is worse than one scoped honestly.
>
> **First walked 2026-08-10, and it did not run.** Four defects, all in items already marked ✅, fixed in R-036b above. The lesson worth keeping: every one of them was invisible to the item that introduced it and obvious the moment somebody followed the tenant, the vendor and the PM through in one sitting. Walk the checkpoint when the milestone closes, not when it is convenient.

## Milestone 3: The money loop (Golden Path 2)

Leases, the append-only ledger, Stripe, autopay, jurisdiction-correct late fees, and the rent roll the owner actually opens on Monday.

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 33 | ✅ R-033 | Lease records: parties (tenants + guarantors with distinct roles), term, rent, deposit held, utility responsibility, status machine (pending / active / notice-given / ended), MTM rollover rate; **inherited-tenant intake path** for acquisitions (no application on file, unknown deposit status, condition-as-found baseline) | PRD LEASE-06, RISK-08 | M | R-009, R-002 | MVP |
| 34 | ✅ R-034 | **Stripe Billing foundation** (D-11): Customer per tenant/payer, Price per lease, Subscription anchored to the lease's billing day computed in **property-local** time, SetupIntents for saved methods, signed webhooks with idempotency keys, and the **webhook → projection** pipeline that every later financial screen reads. Underwriting application submitted at R-001, not here — multi-LLC KYB has a real lead time | PRD §7; D-11 | L | R-002, R-006 | MVP |
| 35 | ✅ R-035 | **Ledger projection + money rules in core** (D-11, D-12): an append-only `LedgerEntry` projection built from Stripe invoice and payment webhooks — never written directly, and reconciled against Stripe on a schedule with drift alarmed, because a projection nobody checks is a lie with a timestamp. Alongside it, the calculations Stripe cannot express: late-fee amount **and its state-cap clamp**, proration in both directions, RUBS allocation, payment-allocation policy from R-010, `daysPastDue` from the original due date. All unit-tested in `packages/core` | PRD PAY-03; D-11, D-12 | L | R-033, R-010, R-034 | MVP |
| 36 | ✅ R-036 | Subscription lifecycle: create on lease activation, update on rent change or renewal (R-065), pause/resume, cancel on move-out; **billing-cycle anchor derived in property-local time**; a Billing Runs screen reading Stripe's invoice events with per-lease outcomes and a re-sync action for drift. Much smaller than a hand-rolled scheduler — this is the item where D-11 actually pays off | PRD PAY-03, NOTIF-03 | M | R-035, R-034 | MVP |
| 36b | ✅ R-036b | **Golden Path 1 repair** — four defects found by walking the demo checkpoint end to end for the first time, none of them in the item that surfaced them. `VERIFIED` was in neither `OPEN_STATUSES` nor the vendor link's `ACTIONABLE_STATUSES`, so the tenant's one tap deleted the job from every screen and killed the vendor's link before the invoice arrived; nothing ever closed the originating `Ticket`, so every later text from that tenant threaded onto the fixed job forever; the entry window was parsed in the **server's** timezone and rendered in the **property's**, so a served legal notice stated hours nobody intended to keep; and `reapprovalCheck` was wired only to the hand-typed-actuals path, so a vendor's invoice could close a job at any multiple of what the owner approved. Plus a `workorder_ready_to_close` Task, because "yes, it's fixed" is where a job stops being maintenance and starts being money | PRD MAINT-04, MAINT-05, MAINT-07; D-6, D-9, D-19 | S | R-030, R-027, R-026 | MVP |
| 37 | ✅ R-037 | Tenant payments: ACH free, card with a disclosed pass-through fee, **retail cash network** for cash-preferring tenants; Stripe-hosted fields only (no card or bank data ever stored); pending → settled states from Stripe; receipts on the tenant's preferred channel; balance and itemized charges visible before paying. **Collection-method split gated on OQ-11** — Stripe's Hosted Invoice Page cannot take partial payments, so the tenant-facing payment UI is ours regardless of how OQ-11 lands | PRD PAY-01; OQ-11 | M | R-034, R-035, R-018 | MVP |
| 37a | R-037a | **Retail cash network** (PAY-01's last unbuilt clause) — **GATED ON A VENDOR DECISION, and on nothing else.** Stripe has no US retail-cash rail and waiting for one is not a plan — it offers the pattern elsewhere (Konbini, OXXO) and not here, and under D-30 the US is the only market. Cash App Pay is US but is a *wallet* funded by a Cash App balance or a linked debit card — a different persona from the tenant who deals in physical notes, and **rejected 2026-08-10** because it would have to be absorbed rather than passed through (see the surcharge note below). Real options are third parties: PayNearMe, VanillaDirect (Green Dot), CheckFreePay. Each needs a contract, a fee schedule, and a settlement webhook or file to reconcile against — none of which can be designed before one is chosen. The seam is already built and waiting: `PaymentRail` and `PaymentChannel` both carry `RETAIL_CASH`, and the tenant is shown the option as unavailable *with a reason* rather than having it hidden. **Do not build a simulated driver for this** (D-15): a cash rail that mints fake settlements is untested code that looks finished, against a vendor whose actual settlement shape is unknown. Until it lands, R-038 carries the cash-preferring tenant | PRD PAY-01; D-15 | M | R-037, vendor decision | MVP |
| 37b | ✅ R-037b | **Surcharge compliance: debit vs credit.** The card pass-through built in R-037 applies to any `card` payment, but several states bar surcharging **debit** specifically — Tex. Bus. & Com. Code §604A.003 among them — and the funding type is not known at the moment the fee is quoted and disclosed. Stripe's own answer is a surcharge-provider app (Yeeld, InterPayments) that computes a compliant amount from the card's brand, funding type and issuing country; its automatic-surcharge product is US-only and in preview. Either integrate one, or make `cardSurchargePermitted` a three-state rule (credit-only / all / none) and quote the fee after the payment method is attached rather than before — which trades a compliance risk for a worse disclosure, since PAY-01 wants the number shown *before* the choice. **Needs the attorney review D-4 already requires, not just an engineering call**. **DONE 2026-08-25 (D-149)**: took the three-state route - `CardSurchargePolicy` is `NONE`/`CREDIT_ONLY`/`ALL` and `cardFeeFor` takes the card's funding type, refusing a CREDIT_ONLY surcharge on anything not known to be credit. The surcharge-provider route was not taken (a preview, US-only, third-party product for a fee this portfolio may simply forgo), and **neither was "quote the fee after the payment method is attached"** - PAY-01 wants the number before the choice, so the quote passes `unknown` and Texas therefore charges no card surcharge at all until the flow can learn the funding type. **The item's real find was a bug it would otherwise have detonated**: `railsFor` was being handed `cardSurchargePermitted` as its `cardPermitted` flag, so the moment a jurisdiction stopped permitting the surcharge, card payments were turned off entirely. Attorney review is still owed and `reviewedBy` is still null | PRD PAY-01; D-4, D-12 | M | R-037 | MVP |
| 37c | ✅ R-037c | **Test isolation: portfolio-wide staff bleed across concurrent test files.** `escalation.test.ts` fails roughly one unit run in two with `Field staffUser is required to return data, got null` from `onCallStaffForProperty`. Not a product bug — no orphaned rows exist, the `staffUserId` FK is RESTRICT, and R-004 deactivates staff rather than deleting them. | 44 | ✅ R-044 | **"Which bucket" and "past grace" are different questions, and the whole item is shaped around not conflating them.** The 0–5 / 6–15 / 16–30 / 30+ buckets are counted flat from the due date; *past grace* is a legal line that moves by state (`JurisdictionRule.graceDays`, D-4). Texas grants **one** day — so a tenant one day late is visibly delinquent on the report AND must not be chased, and treating bucket membership as chaseable is how a rent-roll screen becomes a fair-housing complaint. They are computed separately, never derived from one another, `graceDays: null` never resolves to "chase them", and grace is strictly-greater-than so the last day is still inside it. Aged from the **oldest** unpaid charge, so a tenant who paid this month while March is outstanding reads as months late rather than current. Rent roll carries rent, balance, days late, autopay, deposit held as a liability and subsidy portion, in **four bulk reads** rather than a query per lease. CSV export is injection-guarded (a lender opens it in Excel) while leaving negative numbers summable. The bulk chase sends to an **explicit selection**, re-checks past-grace server-side, skips any recipient with an unfillable merge field rather than posting a raw token, authorises **each lease against its own property**, and is idempotent per lease per day. **Found along the way:** three resource-less permission checks — page, export route and send action — each of which denied the property-scoped manager whose job this screen is. **Left behind:** a courtesy reminder before grace has no path here; date-range filtering and PDF are RPT-02's remainder | PRD PAY-06, RPT-02; D-4, D-9, D-11, D-13, D-44 | M | R-035, R-049 | MVP |

Cut rather than deferred, on D-121's reasoning and R-095/R-096's precedent: a row nobody will build reads as work waiting to be done. **The schema survives untouched, which is why this costs nothing and reverses cleanly** — D-13 deliberately took the low-regret option in R-002 and shipped `LeasePayer` + `PayerAllocation` while deferring the features, so a voucher lease already has somewhere to live. Every ordinary lease is one `LeasePayer` carrying the whole charge and allocation always runs: **one code path, not two**, so nothing in the product is carrying dead weight for a case that will not come. **What was cut is the voucher FEATURES**: recertification proration on effective dates, one bulk HAP deposit reconciled across many tenant projections, housing authority / caseworker / HAP contract / inspection dates / abatement status, dunning that never pursues the HAP portion, and failed-HQS auto-work-orders against the re-inspection deadline. **Re-opens on one fact**: the first voucher tenancy — and it re-opens as a build against a schema that already fits, which is the whole point of D-13 having been taken as a schema decision rather than a feature one. The expensive retrofit this row's own risk entry warned about can no longer happen either way | PRD PAY-13; D-136 | L | R-035, R-024 | ~~MVP*~~ cut |
| 50b | ✅ R-050b | **Golden Path 2 repair** — walking Demo checkpoint 2 end to end (D-28) found that `assessLateFees()` could not see ordinary rent at all. D-11/D-40 mint a `Charge` row for rent only in the exceptions (a move-in proration); ordinary subscription-billed rent (month two onward, the common case for every lease past its first month) posts as an unlinked ledger entry with nothing dated to query. `rentRoll()`'s aging already had this exact gap and was fixed at R-044/R-045 by falling back to a `rentDueDay`-derived date; the late-fee assessor was never given the same fix, so **the late-fee engine silently did nothing for the common case, indefinitely.** The real fix needed a schema change, not a query tweak: a late fee's `assessedOnChargeId` is a real foreign key, and unlinked rent has no `Charge` row to anchor to. Added `Charge.assessedOnLeaseId` + `assessedForDueOn` (the second field because unlinked rent is one lease-wide balance that can represent a different overdue cycle each time it's checked - without it, a fee assessed against a paid-off March debt would be read as already covering a completely unrelated May debt). `assessLateFees` now runs two passes: the original per-charge loop, unchanged; and a new per-lease pass for leases that never got a dated RENT charge at all, reusing `delinquencyFor()` (the same function `rentRoll()` reads from, so the two can never disagree) rather than re-deriving grace/bucket logic. Verified two ways: 11 unit tests (delta correctness, idempotency, TX's cap, and that a later distinct cycle isn't silently netted against an earlier already-assessed one), and a full Golden Path 2 walk against the simulator - rent posts on two leases, autopay collects one, the other goes unpaid, the fix fires a correct capped late fee on grace+1, the pay-now link resolves to the right payer, a completed payment halts the ladder, and the owner dashboard (scoped to one LLC) shows all of it. **Found and left for a named follow-up, not fixed here:** the grace-period reminder (`sendReminders()`, the rent-roll bulk chase) still mints no pay-now link — only the pre-grace due-date reminder does (R-046's own note already flagged this) | PRD PAY-04, RPT-01; D-4, D-11, D-12, D-28 | M | R-050 | MVP |

Four existing pages gained `searchParams` filters (`rent-roll?pastGrace=1`, `maintenance?glowing=1`, `leases?expiresWithin=90|120`, `tasks?type=workorder_approval`); two pages are new (`/vacancies`, `/renewals`), neither had a cross-property list view before this item. **D-46: "Renewals & alerts," not "compliance"** — the tile reads `filingCabinetAlertsDue()` (mortgage/insurance dates only, written at R-015, never called until now), and both the tile and its page say so, because the real statutory compliance calendar is R-077's and doesn't exist yet. **Found along the way: a real, pre-existing access bug.** The dashboard's own placeholder page and the maintenance list page both used a bare `requirePermission()` — a resource-less RBAC check that only an unrestricted `owner`-role actor can pass — so every property-scoped manager in a multi-entity portfolio hit "You don't have access to that" the moment they signed in, since login redirects to `/dashboard`. Fixed both to `requireScope`, the pattern R-008 already established elsewhere; the same bare-permission pattern is still present on `/money`, `/workorders`, `/search`, `/jurisdiction` and the message-template pages and is flagged rather than silently fixed here. So the row re-opens as a build against a schema that already fits, on the day a first voucher tenancy exists.

> **Demo checkpoint 2 (Golden Path 2).** The nightly run posts rent on a seeded lease → autopay collects one → a second lease goes unpaid → on grace+1 a Texas-correct late fee posts and the reminder fires → a pay-now magic link collects it and halts the ladder → the owner dashboard shows all of it, filtered to one LLC.

## Milestone 4: Notices, evidence & the communication record

The paper trail that wins deposit disputes, evictions, habitability claims and retaliation claims. Built as its own milestone because the requirement is uniform across every module and retrofitting proof-of-delivery is miserable.

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 51 | ✅ R-051 | **Service is more than one event, and the schema could only hold one.** `Notice` has carried `serviceMethod`/`servedAt`/`proofDocumentId`/`trackingNumber` since R-002 and nothing ever wrote the last two — but several states require a notice to vacate be both posted on the door AND mailed, each half with its own proof, so an owner who did the lawful thing could not record it. **`NoticeDelivery`** is one row per service event (**D-47**), append-only by trigger with exactly one permitted mutation: `readAt` may go null → timestamp once, because the tenant opening a portal notice is the only evidence that service reached anybody, and the evidence is when they FIRST read it. Three CHECK constraints hold what each enum value promises — POSTED_WITH_PHOTO requires a photograph, CERTIFIED_MAIL an article number, a read receipt only for PORTAL — in Postgres rather than the action, because a row claiming POSTED_WITH_PHOTO with no photograph is not a weaker record, it is a false one. The old columns stay as the first service and the migration backfills, so history does not start today. **A mechanic is not a permission (D-48):** `NoticeServiceMethod` only ever meant "ways we can record proof", never "lawful here" — `JurisdictionRule.noticeServiceMethods` now says which methods serve which notice type, and `servicePermitted()` answers `true`/`false`/**`null` for "nobody has told us"**, the same call R-044 makes with `graceUnknown`. Choosing a method the state omits WARNS and still records, flagged — refusing would produce no evidence at all, which is worse. The verdict is stored as it stood at service, never recomputed (D-4: rules apply prospectively). Texas seeded from §24.005(f), with **email deliberately absent from the eviction track** because the statute does not name it and an unprovable notice starts a clock nobody can defend. **First PDF path in the repo** (`pdf-lib`, pure JS, no headless browser): chosen over a print stylesheet because the artifact posted on a door must be archived exactly as served — generation is idempotent, since a re-render would produce a different file for the same served notice. Wrapping and pagination are the renderer's job and both are tested, including that a long notice grows past one page rather than being silently clipped. Plus the first notice screens, staff and tenant. **Found along the way:** a recurring iCloud-sync hazard that had resurrected `verify-link 2.ts` (already deleted once at R-046), duplicated 167 files, committed two `.claude/settings 2.json` copies back at R-029/R-039a, and corrupted the esbuild binary twice mid-session; plus two bugs in this item's own tests — a hardcoded fixture date that was genuinely in the future for a Chicago property past UTC midnight, and a success-banner assertion that raced the action it was meant to wait for. **Left behind:** TCPA consent is **R-051b** (split deliberately — STOP was already built at R-030); no admin matrix for a new state's service methods; `Notice` itself still mutable; no certified-mail API (**R-093** owns it — this row said R-081, which is the reporting item and never owned a vendor driver; corrected when R-081 was split) | PRD COMM-02; D-4, D-47, D-48 | L | R-049, R-010, R-012 | MVP |
| 51b | ✅ R-051b | **The product had revocation and never had permission.** `SmsOptOut` has honoured STOP since R-030, so the only gate on outbound SMS was withdrawal of a permission nothing ever established — backwards under a statute whose damages are per-message. **`TenantConsent` records the BASIS, not a boolean** (**D-49**): who agreed, how, shown what words, recorded by whom. `EXPRESS_WRITTEN` is the only basis that reaches promotional sending and carries a CHECK constraint requiring the disclosure text — the basis that unlocks marketing is the one that must be able to show what was agreed to. `EXISTING_RELATIONSHIP`/`VERBAL`/`IMPORTED` cover tenancy messages only. Append-only by trigger except withdrawal (`revokedAt` null → timestamp, once, with a required reason), the same shape R-051 gave `NoticeDelivery`. **Keyed on the TENANT, not the number**, so a reassigned number cannot inherit the previous tenant's consent and a tenant who changes number does not lose it — which one table keyed on either alone cannot express. **The existing roster is grandfathered** as `EXISTING_RELATIONSHIP`/`BACKFILL` with a note saying plainly that nobody clicked anything: gating without it would have silently switched off rent reminders and notice delivery for every current tenant on ship day, a worse outcome than the risk removed, and the row claims nothing false. Send path suppresses with a new **`no_consent`** reason, deliberately distinct from `sms_opt_out` (a carrier fact about a NUMBER the tenant can reverse) and from `preference_off` (a choice they never made) — the three have different fixes. Staff and vendor SMS is ungated: neither is the residential consumer the regime is written about, and gating them would stop the on-call pager for nothing. `PROMOTIONAL_CATEGORIES` ships **empty on purpose** — every category today is transactional, and the set exists so the first one that isn't has to declare itself rather than inheriting a basis that does not cover it. **Left behind:** `SmsOptOut` carries the same evidentiary weight and still has no append-only guard; there is still no tenant-onboarding form in the product, so "captured at onboarding" has nowhere to live and capture is a staff action on the lease screen | PRD COMM-02; D-4, D-49 | M | R-051, R-030 | MVP |
| 52 | ✅ R-052 | **COMM-05's "immutable audit log" already existed; the gap was a READ.** `Message`, `Notification`, `NoticeDelivery` and `AuditLog` are each append-only by trigger (R-002/R-016/R-051), so a fifth table would have duplicated evidence into a second place that can drift from the first — the exact ambiguity an evidence trail exists to prevent. What had never happened is reading them TOGETHER: a thread transcript showed `Message` rows only, omitting every automated notification, which for an ordinary tenancy is most of what was ever sent. **A packet showing a tenant's complaints but none of our eleven reminders argues their case, not ours.** `packages/core/comms/record.ts` merges the three sources and writes nothing. **The document never claims more than the evidence supports**: `SENT` prints as "accepted by the provider", not "delivered" (R-040e's distinction, and the transcript's own authority is what would make the overstatement persuasive); an outbound entry with no delivery row says so rather than rendering blank; an unrecognised suppression reason prints raw rather than being guessed at; an empty transcript states that it is empty. **PAY-09's statement is not a filter over the period** — `statementForPeriod()` runs the balance across the whole tenancy and windows it afterwards, because filtering first starts every period at zero and a March statement for a tenant who entered owing $500 would close $500 light with every line arithmetically correct: internally consistent, materially false, nothing on its face revealing it. "No cryptic codes" is treated as a requirement about the READER — types spelled out, `$25.00 CR` with a footnote instead of a minus sign, a reversal that says it corrects an earlier line while the corrected line stays visible (D-11 forbids tidied history). Money columns align via a new `mono` block drawn in Courier, padded in core where the layout is assertable character-by-character. **D-50: Stripe's own invoice PDFs are embedded** (`getInvoicePdf` + `appendPdfs`), and every failure degrades to a NAMED gap — silence about a missing attachment reads as "there was no invoice", a different and false claim — with the statement re-rendered before archiving if an invoice arrives but will not parse. The simulator returns `null`, never a fabricated PDF. **Both exports are archived rather than regenerated**, deliberately unlike `generateNoticePdf`: the source rows are immutable but they keep ARRIVING, so a transcript taken in March and regenerated in June is a different document and "which one did we give the attorney" would have no answer. Neither is linked to the tenant — a packet for a dispute with them must not land in their own portal. R-051's renderer was generalized, not copied. **Found:** a bug in this item's own first draft (the continuation line that exists to print what did not fit was itself truncated, caught by its test); `friendlyTimestamp` under `en-GB` labels an American zone `GMT-5` rather than `CDT`, useless on a Texas exhibit; and 10 duplicate Prisma engine binaries (190 MB) from the pre-move iCloud sync, which failed all six e2e specs in ~400ms each — the environmental signature, not a code one. **Left behind:** R-032's work-order timeline still has no PDF though its own header defers it here (thread-scope vs incident-scope, and its backlog line does not name it); the embedding path is exercised only against live Stripe; no page index for appended invoices | PRD COMM-05, PAY-09; D-11, D-50 | M | R-017, R-005, R-035 | MVP |
| 53 | ✅ R-053 | **"Per-recipient delivery status" already had a home.** `Notification`/`NotificationDelivery` (R-016) already is one row per recipient per channel with a real status — a fifth table would have duplicated evidence R-052 already treats as the single source. What this item added was a way to reach a SEGMENT rather than a reviewed list of leases. **A segment is a filter, not R-044's explicit selection, and that is a deliberate difference, not a shortcut**: the bulk chase reviews a screen of names because "past grace" can go stale between render and press; an announcement has nothing to go stale — broadcasting to a filter *is* the feature. Resolved server-side against `propertyWhere(scope)` (the same helper every scoped list already uses) intersected with the segment, so a crafted `segmentValue` can never reach outside the actor's own scope. **`notify()` refuses when a template's fixed category disagrees with the call's category** (the check that keeps a tenant's rent-reminder mute from silently also muting something else), so `comms.managed_template` — hardcoded to `rent_reminder` since R-044 — could not carry this; registered a second carrier, `comms.announcement`, under a new `announcement` category, same render function. **Property gained `metro`/`tags`** (freeform strings, no canonical geography or tag taxonomy) — invented scaffolding a 10–50 unit portfolio does not need; two properties spelling a metro differently just fail to group, which is visible on the segment picker rather than silently wrong. **Found and fixed as part of this item, not deferred:** a label that WRAPS its `<select>` gets an accessible name built from the control's own rendered option text, not just the label text — the segment-type select's "One property" option collided under `getByLabel('Property')` with the global property/entity switcher's own label, caught by this item's own e2e spec. Fixed by using `SelectField`'s existing sibling-label pattern (`htmlFor`/`id`) instead of hand-rolling one, which is the reuse rung of the ladder doing double duty as the accessibility fix. **Found, flagged, not fixed:** `nsf-fees.test.ts` and `jurisdiction/queries.test.ts` both independently chose the literal state code `'YY'` as their "nothing configured" placeholder; run in parallel workers against the shared test database, one can observe the other's still-live fixture before its own `afterAll` cleanup runs — reproduced once, passed clean on rerun, and is a test-isolation hazard rather than anything this item's own code touches. **Left behind:** setting metro/tags is one property at a time on the edit form — no bulk-tag tool; no "how many will this reach" preview before sending, only the result after; persistent announcement history beyond the immediate per-send result table is R-054's | PRD COMM-04 | S | R-049 | MVP |

Prop. Code §92.332(a)'s six months), every other state honestly silent rather than guessing. **Wired into the two actions that are ALREADY real today, not into R-065/R-066's not-yet-built UI**: `updateLeaseTerms` when `rentCents` actually RISES (a correction or decrease is not the adverse action this guards against), and `recordLeaseNotice` only when `noticeGivenBy === 'LANDLORD'` (a tenant ending their own tenancy is never a retaliation claim). A future R-066 non-renewal Notice calls the same `retaliationCheckFor()` this item built rather than re-deriving it — same "build the shared thing before its second consumer" logic D-9 already established for the Task queue. **Same warn-then-require-a-reason shape as R-027's entry-notice override**: nothing is written until the reason is given, and the acknowledgment is its OWN audit action (`lease.retaliation_window_acknowledged`, in `REASON_REQUIRED`) rather than a flag on `lease.updated`/`lease.notice_given` — `lease.terminated`'s own comment already explains why: `REASON_REQUIRED` cannot express "required only when a warning applied," so whichever action triggers the guard, the record a retaliation defense stands or falls on is the same one query away. **Two real bugs found and fixed while wiring the UI, both in `lifecycle-panel.tsx`'s existing `NoticeForm`, neither in the new guard logic itself**: pinning the `<details>` panel's `open` prop straight to `Boolean(retaliation)` made React re-assert it CLOSED on every render where no warning applied — meaning an ordinary TENANT-given notice (never a warning) would have closed the panel the instant the success state came back, hiding it entirely; fixed by tracking manual-open state and only ever forcing OPEN, never closed. And React 19 resets an uncontrolled field's DOM value once a form action completes (the same fact `ScheduleForm` already documents) — without echoing `state.values` back on the retaliation early return, the rent increase that TRIGGERED the warning would vanish from its own field, and "save anyway" would have silently saved the OLD rent. **An e2e run under this laptop's own load (12+ average, 42 node processes, unrelated stray `playwright test-server` processes from two other projects) produced a wall of uniform 60s timeouts on `mobile-chrome` only** while `desktop-chrome` passed every scenario in under 2s each — CLAUDE.md's own "environment symptom, not a code one" pattern, confirmed by running each project in isolation and by every failure being a generic sign-in redirect timeout, never a retaliation-guard assertion | PRD RISK-06; D-4 | M | R-052, R-051 | MVP |

## Milestone 5: Leasing & tenant lifecycle

Marketing a vacancy through screening, lease execution and renewal. Deep because it carries three external integrations — all simulated first (D-7).

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 56 | ✅ R-056 | **The `Listing` entity itself was the one genuinely new thing — everything it displays is read live from what already existed.** Photos are never copied onto the listing; `unitPhotosForListing()` reads the unit's own `UNIT_PHOTO` library (R-012) at request time, so a photo added or retired on the unit shows up (or disappears) on a live published page without anybody re-uploading. Disclosures are the same call taken one step further: **computed at read time from the property's CURRENT `JurisdictionRule`, never stored** — a listing is a live page, not a signed document (unlike R-051's notices, which archive a frozen PDF on purpose), so a deposit-cap or source-of-income field reviewed *after* publish takes effect on the page immediately rather than requiring a republish. **`JurisdictionRule.sourceOfIncomeProtected`** is the one new jurisdiction field this item actually needed (deposit cap and fee cap already existed) — a nullable `Boolean`, same "null means unreviewed, never guessed" posture R-055's `retaliationWindowDays` just established, seeded `false` for TX statewide with a note that a local ordinance (Austin's, e.g.) would need its own row to override it, the same way any local rule already overrides a statewide one. **A genuinely new kind of public route.** Every existing zero-login page (`vendor/[token]`, `verify/[token]`, `pay/[token]`) is public because a *secret in the path* is the credential; `/listings/[id]` is public because the *record itself* is meant to be public once published, with no secret anywhere in the URL — publication state is the entire authorization, enforced once in `publicListing()` (PUBLISHED-only) and mirrored by a dedicated public photo route rather than reusing `api/documents/[id]/file`, which requires a session and would otherwise send every anonymous visitor to a login wall. A DRAFT or UNPUBLISHED listing 404s exactly like a record outside an actor's scope does everywhere else (ROLE-01) — "not public" and "does not exist" have to read the same, or the response itself would confirm a guessed draft id is real. **Two real e2e-authoring bugs found and fixed, neither in the shipped feature**: the create-redirect wait matched `.../listing/new` itself (the create page's own URL) and captured the literal string `"new"` as a listing id — the identical trap `leases.spec.ts`'s own `capturedLeaseId` already documents, missed because this was a new file rather than copied from one that already had the guard; and a "pets allowed requires policy text" test tried to assert a *server-rendered* validation message that a native HTML `required` attribute blocks the browser from ever reaching — the field's own `required` is doing its job, the test's assumption was wrong, not the app, and the server-side check is still proven at the correct layer (`packages/core/listings/listings.test.ts`, not e2e) | PRD LEASE-01 | M | R-009, R-012, R-010 | MVP |
| 57 | ✅ R-057 | **The first of D-7's three named simulated adapters actually built** (`SimulatedScreeningAdapter`/`SimulatedESignAdapter` are still R-060/R-063), so there was no sibling to copy — generalized instead from `SimulatedBillingProvider`'s own precedent: mint ids in a realistic shape, log every call, hold **no state of its own**. `ListingSyndication` is where D-27 actually earns its keep here — `externalId`/`lastFaultCode` record what the simulated network *told us*, never a mirror of `Listing.status`, or "is it live on the network" and "did we mean to publish it" would agree by construction and the whole ≤24h-delist path would have nothing independent to prove. **Delist-on-lease-up is two pieces, deliberately split across the transactional boundary**: `lease.activated` (already in the closed event vocabulary, never emitted until now) flips the unit's `PUBLISHED` listing to `UNPUBLISHED` inside the SAME transaction as the lease's own status write; telling each network is a separate hourly sweep that never runs inside that transaction — a provider call inside a database transaction holds a pooled connection open for the length of a third party's outage, the same rule `send.ts` already states for the notification engine, applied here so the shape is already correct for the day a real driver (Phase 3, partner-gated) replaces the simulator. The sweep is self-healing by construction: a failed delist leaves the row exactly as findable next hour as it was this hour, no separate retry bookkeeping. **Lead-source attribution stayed honestly small.** No `Lead`/`Inquiry` entity exists in the schema or the master PRD's own canonical list, and R-058 ("prospect pipeline") is the backlog's own home for one — inventing a rich lead record here would be scope creep into an item two rows down. `ListingLead` is deliberately bare (listing, source, timestamp, nothing else) — proof of which network sent a visitor, for R-058 to build the real pipeline on top of, the same "shared thing before its second consumer" call D-9 already made for the one Task queue. Every network's feed entry gets its own tracked path (`?src=<network>`), which is the entire attribution mechanism | PRD LEASE-02; D-7 | M | R-056 | MVP |
| 58 | ✅ R-058 | Prospect pipeline + **identical auto-sent pre-screening questions to every inquiry** (move date, occupants, pets, income range, prior evictions) — identical is the fair-housing requirement and it also kills most tire-kicker back-and-forth. The five questions are a fixed code notification template, not a managed one — the managed-template system (R-049) renders against a `TenancyRef` a prospect doesn't have, and fair housing requires every inquiry to see the identical wording anyway, so a PM-editable version would be the wrong tool even where it fit. `Prospect` is the deliberate "named person" record R-057's own note promised: `ListingLead` stays the bare anonymous-visit log, and a real form submission (name + email-or-phone) is what creates a `Prospect`, carrying the `?src=` attribution across. Two more auth-token/audit lessons earned the hard way this item: (1) a file mixing a public, session-less action with a staff action that calls `audit()` fails to import under Vitest for every export in the file, because `audit()` transitively pulls in Auth.js — the fix is physically separate files (`actions.ts` public, `staff-actions.ts` staff), not a runtime guard, and it's now the second time this exact split has paid for itself (see R-032c's `verify-link-actions.ts` vs its staff counterpart); (2) `next/headers`'s `headers()` throws outside a real request scope under Vitest with no workaround, so any action reading it (here, `submitInquiry`'s IP-based rate limit) is e2e-only by construction, documented in the test file's own header rather than discovered fresh next time. Two real bugs, both caught by e2e rather than any inline review: the prescreen success screen never rendered because `submitPrescreenAnswers` wrote the DB but never called `revalidatePath`, so the Server Component reading `prescreenLinkStatus()` had no reason to refetch after the client-side `useActionState` update; and the admin detail page's three-way ternary checked `preScreenSentAt` (was an invite ever sent) before `preScreenRespondedAt` (did they actually answer), so a prospect who answered showed "Not sent yet." instead of their answers whenever the two facts diverged — fixed to gate on `answered` first. Local e2e traffic carries no `x-forwarded-for` header, so `clientIp()`'s fallback resolves to the loopback address (`prospect-inquiry:::1`, not the assumed `:unknown`) and every anonymous inquiry across every run of the spec, in every browser project, shares one `RateLimitCounter` bucket — the spec now clears it by key prefix in `beforeAll`, worth remembering for any future public, IP-rate-limited action's e2e spec. Left behind: no resend control for a bounced/never-answered invite (`sendPrescreenInvite` is exported for exactly this, unused so far); the pipeline stages past `PRE_SCREENED` (showing/applied/screened/approved/signed) are staff-recorded status only, no automation — R-059 and R-064 are where applying and showings actually get built. | PRD LEASE-07 | M | R-057, R-049 | MVP |
| 59 | ✅ R-059 | Online application: one per adult 18+, co-applicant grouping and links, save-and-resume, ID and income document upload, application fee payment with **jurisdiction fee caps**, completion timestamp (which drives order-of-processing). `Application`/`Applicant` are deliberately thin/rich in opposite directions - `Application` is just the household GROUP (property, listing, prospect, one completion timestamp), every real answer lives on `Applicant`, one row per adult, so a household of three is three independently-linked people, not one record wearing three hats. The fee is charged PER APPLICANT, not per household - real SFR practice, and it matches how screening itself runs per-person - snapshotted onto `Applicant.feeCents` at invite time (`applicationFeeCentsFor`, clamped to `JurisdictionRule.applicationFeeCapCents`, D-4/D-12) so a fee change after invite never moves what someone already agreed to owe. Completion is a TWO-FIELD state machine on purpose: `formSubmittedAt` (synchronous, set the moment the form validates) and `completedAt` (set only once the fee also clears OR none was due) are separate fields because a Stripe confirmation is asynchronous, webhook-driven (D-11) - collapsing them into one field would have no honest value to hold while a valid, submitted form waits on a payment in flight. `BillingProvider` widened via two SIBLING methods (`createApplicationFeeCustomer`/`createApplicationFeePaymentIntent`), never by loosening R-037's `leasePayerId`/`leaseId`-required existing methods - the interface's own header already states the rule this follows. The Stripe webhook pipeline is invoice-centric by construction (D-11's ledger projection), so an application fee - no lease, no LeasePayer, never a `LedgerEntry` - is intercepted in a small separate projector (`fee-webhook.ts`) checked BEFORE `billing/webhook.ts`'s own LeasePayer lookup, which would otherwise silently "ignore" the one event that confirms a fee cleared. `APPLICATION_LINK` is a new MULTI-USE token purpose (five days - shorter than TENANT_VERIFY's seven because this one moves money and opens documents, longer than TENANT_PAY_LINK's three because a household needs real time to gather pay stubs and photo IDs), unlike PROSPECT_PRESCREEN's single-use predecessor - `applicationLinkStatus` never rejects on "already answered" the way `prescreenLinkStatus` does, because "come back anytime" is the entire point here. Two real bugs caught only by e2e: a test fixture set `Prospect.status` directly without `preScreenRespondedAt`, silently hiding the ENTIRE Application section the admin page gates on that field, not on status; and a missing wait after clicking "Invite to apply" raced the immediately-following database read against the server action's own commit. Left behind: no resend control for a stalled invite; no cross-application view for staff (folded into the existing `/prospects` pipeline rather than a second list, per that page's own comment); residence history is one current address, not a repeatable timeline; the fee payment UI (Stripe Elements) is proved server-side only, never end-to-end, the identical limitation `AutopayPanel`'s own header already states for the identical reason. | PRD LEASE-03 | L | R-058, R-037 | MVP |

The deposit-defense chain: matched move-in/move-out evidence, a statutory countdown that cannot be missed, and the make-ready project that follows.

R-068 ships online-only unless its own inspection use case justifies building that machinery itself, which is a decision for whoever picks R-068 up. Staff inspecting a vacant unit are usually standing in a house with a phone signal; a tech in a basement was the case that justified it, e-sign or auto-finalize after a stated window, immutable lock on completion. **Shipped in two sessions**: phase 1 (D-57) — checklist templates, per-item condition + notes, the full status machine, a staff-recorded signature, an immutable lock. ## Milestone 7: Reporting, tax, compliance & preventive maintenance

The five reports the owner actually opens, the year-end packet the CPA needs, and the calendar that prevents the quiet failures.
