| 104l | ✅ R-117 | **Walk the demo in a browser, the checkpoint Milestone 11 owed** (**D-28**). R-115 and R-116 each closed a slice of the accessibility audit and neither opened the screens it changed; the milestone's own rule is that a walk catches what no test is looking for. Signed in as a real owner against the local `rental_demo`, 79 staff routes at 1280px **and again at 390px**, the ten portal routes, the public listing and the vendor magic link, with axe on every one. **The automated half came back clean and that is the finding: axe reported zero violations on all 79 staff routes at both widths** - Milestone 11 did what it said - so everything below was found by reading the screens. **(a) The public listing page 500ed three times per view, and it was two defects meeting.** The demo seed wrote unit photos to `<repo>/.data/documents` while the server reads `<repo>/apps/web/.data/documents`: `process.cwd()` is the repo root for a seed run from there and `apps/web` for the Next server, so the identical default expression names two directories. `e2e/portal.spec.ts` already carries a comment about hitting exactly this - *"a 500 that looked like an authorization failure until both `.data` directories turned up on disk"* - and the seed reintroduced it; it now resolves from `import.meta.url` and cannot depend on where it is run. **The 500 is ours, though**: all four byte-serving routes awaited `storage.get` bare, so bytes that are not there were an unhandled throw rather than the deliberate 404 every other refusal on those routes already answers. `documentFileResponse` fetches and answers at the one place bytes meet a browser - the same argument D-137 made about content types at the same four routes - and `MissingStoredObjectError` is a distinct type from both adapters so a Blob outage stays a 500 rather than telling a CDN that a published listing has no photos. **(b) One axe violation in the whole product, and it was only visible on a phone.** `scrollable-region-focusable` on `/portal/pay/history` - a `div.overflow-x-auto` holding only a table has no focusable child, so a keyboard user cannot reach its scrollbar and everything right of the fold is unreachable. Nine wrappers had it and five did not, because `reports/operating` fixed the two it owned and its own comment says why the rest stayed invisible: *the defect does not exist at a desktop width where the table happens to fit*. All fourteen now spread `scrollableRegionProps(label)`. **The suite could not have caught it**: the portal's axe sweep lists four routes and the money screens - the tenant's primary job - were not among them. They are now. **(c) The owner's landing screen said the portfolio had collected 6% of its rent.** *"Collected vs billed $600.00 / $9,400.00"* on 27 August, with the rent roll one click away saying $1,850 outstanding and three of five tenants current. Both numbers were right. Every money plan's newest invoice was `daysAgo: 20`-`30`, so the most recent rent this seed had ever billed was always about a month back, while `collectedVsBilled` is a CALENDAR-MONTH tile whose billed side is the sum of `rentCents` across live leases by design (D-11/D-40). The two never met, on any day the demo was ever walked. Anchored to the 1st of the current month, floored at 8 days so a decline and a second instalment still fit inside it; the tile now reads $7,550 / $9,400 and reconciles exactly against the rent roll's $1,850. **(d) Every vendor visit in the demo was a 7pm-to-10pm evening call.** `daysFrom` sets UTC midnight - correct for a `@db.Date` column, wrong for an appointment - so a work order read *"Scheduled 2026-08-28 19:00 to 22:00"* directly above the entry-notice line that DOES compute property-local (*"the soonest compliant start is 09:26"*). Two clocks on one screen; the page was right both times. `atLocalTime` puts the window at 9am on the property's own clock, and `DEMO_ZONE` names the timezone once so a second zone cannot be added without these being reconsidered. **The real defect found on the way, filed rather than fixed as R-118**: delinquency is aged from every unwaived `Charge` the lease has ever had, paid or not | D-28; PRD §6.4 | M | R-115, R-116 | Milestone 11 |
| 105 | ✅ R-118 | **A tenancy is aged from charges it paid a year ago** (found 2026-08-27 on R-117's walk, deliberately left there because it changes a money and compliance behaviour and wanted its own tests). `rentRoll()` passed every `waivedAt: null` `Charge` into `delinquencyFor`'s `openCharges`, whose contract says *"every charge still contributing to the balance"*. **There is no paid marker on `Charge` to filter by** - under D-11 a `Charge` is the instruction pushed to Stripe and settlement returns as one aggregate balance with no per-charge allocation record - so the filter meant *every charge ever*, `oldestDueOn` took the oldest of them, and `daysLate`, `bucket` and **`pastGrace` all descended from it**. On the demo, Derrick Holt owing exactly this month's $1,650 read **Over 30 days**, aged from a move-in proration due 2025-07-03 and paid on time; Priya Nair, $200 down, read the same from an August 2025 charge. **Not cosmetic**: `pastGrace` gates who may be chased and an older anchor can only make it true EARLIER, so a tenant one day late with any paid charge on file was chaseable on day one whatever the statute allows - the precise failure `aging.ts`'s own header says the module exists to prevent. **The owner chose allocation over deletion (D-151).** The anchor is now COMPUTED, not looked up: the charges plus one debt for the current period's rent (`rentCents` dated `nearestRentDueOn`) sorted newest-first, the balance consumed across them, and the debt the balance runs out on is `oldestDueOn` - because payments settle oldest-first (D-11's allocation order), so what is still owed sits on the newest debts. Rejected: dropping `charges` entirely and aging from `nearestRentDueOn` alone, which is smaller and provably never chases early but makes a genuinely unpaid year-old utility read as at most one month late; and persisting per-charge settlement, which is a migration, a webhook change and a backfill. Allocation gives the same answer as the deletion wherever the deletion is right and keeps real arrears visible where it is not. `openCharges` is renamed `charges` with the opposite contract - EVERY unwaived charge, paid or not - so no caller can quietly re-make the old assumption, and `monthlyRentCents` is **required**: omitting it would leave the rent debt absorbing nothing and restore this exact defect through a missing argument. **The late-fee sweep was never affected and still is not** - it passes `charges: []` by construction, and the two callers disagreeing is what made the defect findable. Three unit tests and one e2e test, and the e2e one was **confirmed to fail against the old anchor before being trusted**: a tenancy one day late on unlinked rent carrying a proration billed and paid 400 days ago, whose two ledger entries net to zero, now reads "1–5 days / still within grace" with no chase control at all | PRD PAY-06, RPT-02; D-4, D-11, D-151 | S | R-044, R-045 | Milestone 11 |

A dead reader is dead code; **a dead writer is a broken behaviour**, and the worst case is a dead writer whose rows a live reader gates on. **(a)** `recordConsent`/`withdrawConsent` are the only writers of `TenantConsent`, `send.ts` has gated every tenant SMS on a consent row since R-051b, and nothing called either — so every tenant SMS in a deployed environment was `SUPPRESSED / no_consent`, permanently, with no screen that could change it. **(b)** `switchCollectionMethod`, the whole of D-29, had no control: nobody could be put on a payment plan or taken off autopay. Both wired rather than deleted, both reading their id from the form. Found on the way: both consent actions revalidated `/leases` instead of `/leases/[id]`; `allocatePayment` (D-11/D-12's allocation order, configurable in the jurisdiction form) is dead too and needs an owner decision; and the item's own success spec caught the sixth `/leases/[id]` label collision, this one between a status line and its own new `<option>`. | PRD 00 §13, COMM-02, D-29 | M | R-138, R-142 | 2 |

What the projection did instead was settle every charge the invoice named IN FULL and give the leftover to rent, which is fee-before-rent under any order — including the RENT-first one Texas is seeded with, and rent still reading unpaid is what a pay-or-quit notice is served on. **The rent debt has no `Charge` row** (D-11/D-40), so allocating over charge rows alone would have left that defect exactly where it was; it is represented the way `delinquencyFor` already represents it (R-118/D-151). **Second half**: when the named charges totalled more than what arrived the projection wrote ONE UNLINKED row, so each of them went on reading as fully outstanding on the tenant's pay screen for ever while the balance said otherwise — the `!fits` fallback whose own comment deferred to *"R-035's allocation policy"*. Found on the way: **`outstandingCharges` was dead too** and is now wired, and neither it nor the pay screen's hand-rolled copy of it excluded a **waived** charge, so a forgiven fee could absorb a payment between the waiver and its credit note's webhook | PRD PAY-03; D-4, D-11, D-12, D-151; R-143's leftover | S | R-035, R-143 | Milestone 11 |

## Milestone 12: Arc 2 — the operator's second pass

Sourced from the 2026-09-02 operator review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-02-operator-review.md`), commissioned by D-164 after the original backlog closed at R-152. Ordering: the five findings where the product's behaviour is **wrong** come first, then the rest by operational pain × frequency. Two rows are OQ-gated and say so. The review's **"Do not build" list is part of this milestone**: no second work queue for cases (D-9), no two-way calendar sync, no settings UI for house-rule constants, no dark mode / quarterly columns / VTIMEZONE, no backfill of R-038a's historical no-ledger payments. Each row's claims are inherited evidence — re-verify against the code before building (R-150's lesson: the inherited list is never trusted).

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 140 | ✅ R-153 | **WRONG — editing a jurisdiction rule silently wipes nine statutory fields.** `createRuleVersion` writes only what the rule form renders; `noticeServiceMethods`, the three abandonment periods, `leaseViolationCureDays`, the NSF pair and the card-surcharge pair all fall to schema defaults on every new version — an uncapped NSF fee starts posting, D-92's refusal makes belongings disposal impossible portfolio-wide, and the audit row records too little to see the loss afterwards. Fix: spread the previous version's row and overlay the form's fields; render the missing nine (service-method map as its own sub-form). Test: v2 from a fully-populated v1 through the action, every column equal or deliberately changed. Review finding 1 | D-4, D-48, D-92; review §1 | M | R-148 | Arc 2 |
| 141 | ✅ R-154 | **WRONG — a renewed tenancy has no deposit, so no disposition clock ever starts.** Renewal copies `depositCents` as a figure and leaves the `Deposit` row on the predecessor lease (D-54); every reader keys off `lease.deposits`, so a renewed tenancy gets no `dispositionDueOn`, no Task, no statutory letter, and the rent roll prints $0.00 held. R-128 fixed the demo symptom by seeding, not the cause. Fix: re-point the `Deposit` to the successor at renewal cutover (one writer, one place). Assert `startDepositDisposition` returns `started` on a twice-renewed lease, and the demo seed gains a renewed tenancy so the walk can see it. Review finding 2 | D-54; review §2 | M | R-071, R-064 | Arc 2 |
| 142 | ✅ R-155 | **WRONG — `certifiedFundsOnly` is not enforced at the counter, only online.** `offlinePaymentDecision` never reads the flag (the offline reader does not even select the column), so a tenancy under legal action can pay by personal cheque or cash at exactly the moment the switch says cashier's cheque or money order only — R-038a's recorded gap, carried since. Fix: `certifiedFundsOnly` + instrument into `OfflineFacts`; refuse `OFFLINE_CHECK` and `CASH` with the existing neutral-sentence pattern. One core test per instrument. Review finding 4 | review §4 | S | R-038a | Arc 2 |
| 143 | ✅ R-156 | **WRONG — nothing connects money arriving to a notice or an eviction case.** A payment during the cure window moves the case toward FILING with no warning; serving a pay-or-quit neither places nor prompts for the payment hold that lives on a different screen (R-047's open end, not closed by R-083). Fix: serve-and-hold inline on `CURE_NOTICE_TYPES` service; `cureClock()` takes payments-since-service and the case page shows a red band (amount, date, channel, acceptance-may-waive); the packet's exhibit index lists any such payment. **OQ-gated**: whether and how much acceptance waives is state law → new `JurisdictionRule` fields with counsel-reviewed values, never code. Review finding 3 | R-047, R-083; review §3 | M | R-155, R-153 | Arc 2 |
| 144 | ✅ R-157 | **WRONG — inspections enter occupied units with no entry notice.** `entryDecision` has three callers and no inspection path is one of them — the auto-scheduled annual interior walk enters with no notice, illegal-entry exposure that taints the very photos a deposit case rests on (R-073 named it; nothing owned it). Fix: route inspection scheduling through the same `entryDecision` → generate → serve → log chain work orders use; a walk cannot be marked performed without one — warn-and-override with a reason (R-027's posture), never a hard block. Review finding 5 | PRD INSP-01; R-073; review §5 | M | R-070, R-020 | Arc 2 |
| 145 | ✅ R-158 | **One stall sweep for the five case types that have none** — accommodation/ESA response clock (fair-housing, D-89 refuses to pause it, so unwatched is worst), abandonment attempts gone quiet, violation cure expired unserved, insurance claim silent past the 24h mitigation target, party-change amendment unsigned with an unscreened occupant. One `case-stall-job.ts`, per-type thresholds as named constants with reasoning beside them, raising the existing `Task` type — D-9 forbids a second queue. Accommodation escalates rather than nudges. Review finding 7 | D-9, D-89; review §7 | M | R-086–R-090 | Arc 2 |
| 146 | ✅ R-159 | **Court-date reminders.** `EvictionCase.courtDate` is stored, displayed, and never scheduled against — a missed hearing loses by default at the highest cost-per-occurrence in the product. One `SCHEDULED_JOBS` entry: Tasks at T-7 and T-1 property-local, plus a Task when a hearing is within a week and the DMDC search is >30 days stale (R-085's render-only warning made actionable). Review finding 9 | R-083, R-085; review §9 | S | R-158 | Arc 2 |
| 147 | ✅ R-160 | **Move-out proration** — `moveInProration`'s mirror, deferred twice and never landed; a tenancy ending mid-month is billed the full month and argues it against the deposit letter. `moveOutProration` in core on the lease's own `prorationMethod`, pushed as a credit invoice item when `moveOutAt` is recorded, so the disposition's "minus balance owed" is a settled number. Review finding 10 | D-12; review §10 | S | R-071 | Arc 2 |
| 148 | ✅ R-161 | **`Notice` becomes append-only by trigger**, like the other four evidence tables — flagged in R-051, carried through R-052, mutable ever since. Correction is a superseding `Notice` linked to the one it replaces; the served original is never overwritten. Cheap now, expensive after the first backfill. Review finding 13 | R-051; review §13 | S | R-052 | Arc 2 |
| 149 | ✅ R-162 | **The second state becomes configurable without a seed script.** The rule form renders every statutory field (R-153 adds the nine); a "start a state from an existing one" clone flow with `citation`/`reviewedBy` required before it can go effective; a portfolio screen naming which states have no rule (seeded FL properties have none — `rulesFor()` throws) and which have unreviewed fields, absorbing R-055's retaliation-window gap list. The legal-review release gate (backlog flagged-gaps §6) applies. Review finding 11 | D-4; review §11 | M | R-153 | Arc 2 |
| 150 | ✅ R-163 | **The reconciliation learns to compare amounts.** D-25's sweep checks the projection against our own event log, so an event Stripe never sent is invisible on both sides — the exact defect R-038a found live. Fetch each open invoice's `amount_due`/`amount_paid` on the nightly sweep, compare to the projected lease balance, alarm the difference on R-147's existing drift surface, never correct it. Review finding 14 | D-25; review §14 | M | R-147 | Arc 2 |
| 151 | ✅ R-164 | **`/portal/account` — the tenant gets a say.** Notification preferences (locked categories explained, per NOTIF-02), the TCPA consent shown with its basis and a withdraw control writing a new `TenantConsent` row, autopay off through `switchDecision` (D-29/D-36 already refuse the email-less case), digest opt-in — plus the staff-side mirror for the counter. Today `recipientType` is hardcoded `'STAFF'` and no tenant has ever had a preference set. Review finding 8 | PRD NOTIF-02; D-29, D-36; review §8 | M | R-051b, R-037 | Arc 2 |
| 152 | ✅ R-165 | **The guarantor becomes a person who can see what they owe.** Seeded role, no auth path (R-004's leftover), no view, no release. Magic-link entry to a financial-only view (balance, ledger, notices — no maintenance, no comms, per ROLE-01); the demand ladder addressed to them alongside the tenant; release through a signed `LeasePartyChange`, never an unsigned delete. Review finding 15 | PRD ROLE-01; review §15 | M | R-090, R-046 | Arc 2 |
| 153 | ✅ R-166 | **Counter receipt and cheque batching** — R-038's two named leftovers. Print-ready receipt from core's existing document generation on the record-payment confirmation; a batch screen grouping undeposited offline payments by date and receiver into a printable deposit slip, batch id on each `Payment` so a bank line reconciles to named tenancies. Review finding 16 | R-038; review §16 | S | R-038 | Arc 2 |
| 154 | ✅ R-167 | **Vacancy loss in dollars and economic occupancy.** Nothing prices a vacant day; `occupancyRate` has zero callers and counts `DOWN` as vacant. A vacancy-loss line (market rent × vacant days) on `/reports/property` and the rent-roll footer; economic occupancy = collected ÷ (scheduled + vacancy loss + concessions), `DOWN` excluded from the denominator and said so. Retire or rewrite `occupancyRate` — no second definition left around. Review finding 12 | PRD RPT-05; R-075; review §12 | S | R-081a | Arc 2 |
| 155 | ✅ R-168 | **Import tooling, half (i) — entities, tenants, leases and documents** (PRD §6.8, adoption-critical, never had a row). One CSV, one row per (tenant, lease) pairing — a lease with roommates is two rows sharing the same property/unit/start date, which is what lets a plain spreadsheet describe a multi-tenant tenancy with no repeating-group column. LegalEntity matched by name, Property by address (`addressComparisonKey`), Unit by (property, name); Tenant is NEVER matched, always a fresh row, on the same "never guess" reasoning `party-change-builder.ts` already gives. A dry-run plan (`packages/core/import`) runs against a fresh DB snapshot at both preview and commit — commit refuses unless every row is clean, no partial-skip. Every lease is written `origin: 'INHERITED'`, `depositTransferStatus: 'UNKNOWN'` (deposits at a KNOWN-POSITION status, not assumed) and raises the existing inherited-lease intake tasks — exactly the machinery R-033 already built for an acquired tenancy, reused rather than re-invented. No jurisdiction deposit-cap check on import: the deposit was already collected under whatever rule applied when it was, and re-applying today's cap to a historical fact would ask an owner to shrink money already held. Bulk document upload keyed by address + type reuses `uploadDocument`'s own write path (storage, sha256, EXIF capture time, closed DOCUMENT_TYPES vocabulary) so an imported document is never second-class. New `Property.historyStartsOn` — reports (today: the operating snapshot's vacancy floor) suppress vacancy/income conclusions before it, distinct from `acquiredOn`. **Half (ii), opening balances, is split off as R-168a below — it needs an owner decision on the balance mechanism before it starts**, so it could not be built in the same item without deciding that silently. Review finding 6 | PRD §6.8; D-11; review §6 | L | R-153–R-160 | Arc 2 |
| 155a | ✅ R-168a | **Import tooling, half (ii) — opening balances.** OQ resolved by the owner (D-170): one lump Stripe invoice item per payer, amount is the NET balance at migration. Two new optional CSV columns (`opening_balance_dollars`, `opening_balance_as_of`) write a `Charge` (`type: 'OTHER'`) at import commit — unpushed, since a DRAFT imported lease has no Stripe customer yet — and `chargeOpeningBalance` pushes it via `addInvoiceItem` the moment activation provisions one, wired into the same DRAFT→ACTIVE transition that already provisions billing. Never a direct `LedgerEntry` write (D-11). Review finding 6 | PRD §6.8; D-11; D-170; review §6 | M | R-168 | Arc 2 |

## Milestone 13: Arc 3 — money and obligations leaving the building

Sourced from the 2026-09-05 operator review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-05-operator-review.md`), commissioned by D-172 after Arc 2 closed at R-168a. Ordering: the five findings where the product's behaviour is **wrong** come first, then the rest by operational pain × frequency, matching the review's own ranking.

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 156 | ✅ R-169 | **WRONG — the statutory deposit-disposition clock starts a day late for any evening move-out.** `deposit-disposition-start.ts` reads `Lease.moveOutAt` (a real timestamp) with the date-only reader `utcToBusinessDate` instead of `businessDate(instant, zone)` — the identical defect class R-156 already fixed once on the cure clock. Four live call sites: the start itself, the reminder job (skewing the halfway/overdue nudges with it), `turnover/queries.ts`, and `dashboard/queries.ts`, where the correct reader sits two lines away in the same function. Fix: select `property.timezone`, use `businessDate` at all four. **OQ-gated, not part of this fix**: whether the clock should run from `moveOutAt` (staff's click) or the tenant's actual surrender date is state law and an owner/counsel question, not this bug. Review finding 1 | INSP-05, PAY-11; review §1 | S | R-071, R-156 | Arc 3 |
| 157 | ✅ R-170 | **WRONG — the deposit refund is computed, promised in writing, and cannot be paid.** `finalizeDisposition` writes `refundedCents` and a `DEPOSIT_DISPOSITION` `Notice` and stops; there is no `refundPaidAt`, `refundMethod`, cheque number or document link, no refund action, no Task to cut a cheque. The rent roll already reads the deposit as settled once the letter goes out, which is worse evidence than sending nothing if the money never actually moved. Fix: a refund is an event (date, instrument, document) raised as a Task at finalization, and the `Deposit` liability is only released by that event, never by the letter. Review finding 2 | INSP-05, PAY-11; review §2 | M | R-169, R-071 | Arc 3 |
| 157a | ✅ R-170a | **CI has been red on `main` for four consecutive runs and no item caused any of them.** D-171's mobile-chrome pointer-interception races — `e2e/import.spec.ts:323` (bulk document upload) and `e2e/inspections.spec.ts:142`/`:237` (checklist walk, item photo) — fail only under CI's concurrent full-sweep load, pass locally in isolation, and are untouched by R-168a, R-169 and R-170 alike. **The cost is not the three tests, it is that a red pipeline now needs a human to explain it away every push** — the same blindness as the 2026-08-17 dead pipeline wearing the opposite sign (D-171's own entry predicted exactly this reading). Fix: reproduce under CI-like contention rather than in a quiet run (worker count, `mobile-chrome` viewport), then settle whether it is a fixed/sticky element overlapping a bottom-of-viewport control at mobile widths, a scroll-into-view race, or the retry budget — and assert the cause on positive evidence, recording *unknown* rather than guessing if it does not land. Owner decision 2026-09-05: ahead of R-171, because CI's signal is the thing every later row is verified against | D-171; review N/A (pipeline health) | M | R-168a | Arc 3 |
| 158 | ✅ R-171 | **WRONG — every counter payment is counted twice in "collected."** D-169 found `recordOfflinePayment` writes a rich `Payment` row plus a second generic `channel: OTHER` row, and fixed the one consumer that mattered then. Two more have since gone live and both aggregate `Payment` with no channel filter: the dashboard tile and the reports cash summary double every cheque/money-order/cash payment, and the eviction packet's "payments accepted after service" exhibit — which goes to a court — overstates by 100% money whose acceptance may waive a notice (R-156). ~~Fix: filter the aggregate the way `listUndepositedDepositGroups` already does, at all three sites.~~ **That fix was wrong and was not applied** — every invoice-driven ONLINE payment also lands as `channel: OTHER`, so no consumer-side filter can tell the duplicate from real money (D-177). Fixed at the writer instead: `recordOfflinePayment` writes its row before the push, `writePayment` claims it, and all three sites are correct unchanged. Review finding 3 | PAY-05, RPT-01, RPT-05; review §3; D-177 closes D-169 | S–M | R-166, R-156 | Arc 3 |
| 159 | ✅ R-172 | **WRONG — days vacant never stops counting.** `Lease.moveInAt` has no writer anywhere in the codebase, so `getTurnoverForUnit`'s `where: { moveInAt: { not: null } }` never matches, `daysToFill` is never final, and a unit filled last March still reads "179 days vacant and counting" forever. The operating report and leasing funnel dodge it by falling back to `lease.startsOn`; the per-unit turn screen does not. Fix: write `moveInAt` where the tenancy actually starts (move-in inspection completion or key/code issuance, both already logged), and fall back to `startsOn` in `getTurnoverForUnit` the way its siblings do until then. **The READER in `getTurnoverForUnit` is already correct — R-169 fixed it** (`businessDate(moveInAt, timezone)`, not `utcToBusinessDate`); this row owns the WRITER only, and whatever writes `moveInAt` must write a real timestamp for that reader to be right. Review finding 4 | LEASE-12, RPT-05; review §4 | S | R-072, R-167 | Arc 3 |
| 160 | ✅ R-173 | **WRONG — a tenant with no email or phone is recorded as notified through a portal they cannot sign into.** `tenant-magic-link` resolves by email only, both `Tenant.email`/`Tenant.phone` are nullable, and `addressFor` still returns a `SENT` portal delivery for `PORTAL` — "the notification IS the delivery" — for a recipient with no way to reach it. WARNED, never blocked (D-187), and **the re-key waits on nothing**, the one place the checklist order and R-176's urgency genuinely conflict. `cases.stalled` gains its sixth case at `TURN_STALL_DAYS = 6`, reading movement off `WorkOrder.updatedAt`. **Found along the way:** `currentStageFor` — what `/vacancies` prints portfolio-wide — was a third copy of "is this stage done" that disagreed with the other two, so a stage physically finished and merely unpaid still read as the one being worked (D-186). **Left behind:** none of the six `cases.stalled` types links from its Task to the thing it is about; a turn that stalls, resumes and stalls again is flagged once (every R-158 type shares this); inspection findings are still unstaged. | 167 | ✅ R-180 | **A per-entity settlement report** — the cheap half of finding 12. Every LLC's online rent settles into one Stripe balance and one bank account today; the entity boundary every report already respects (R-081a/b/d, D-123, D-168) stops at the money itself, and commingling is the specific thing an LLC exists to prevent. Fix: what settled, for which entity's properties, in a date range, reconcilable to a bank line — so the owner can move funds deliberately between entities and prove they did. **The full answer (a connected account per entity) is explicitly not this row** — it changes every payment path and needs a legal-structure decision (OQ, needs counsel) this review does not have standing to make; re-opens when that decision is made. **The correctness of the item turned out to be which ROWS it is entitled to count, not the arithmetic** (D-191): `Payment.channel` cannot say whether money went through Stripe — the webhook writes `OTHER` for every invoice-driven payment because an invoice event carries no `rail`, so a `CARD`/`ACH` filter drops most online rent — and the discriminator is `receivedByStaffId`, the same one the webhook itself uses, whose only writer anywhere is `recordOfflinePayment`. An offline check is excluded for a reason of substance: it reaches Stripe as an OUT-OF-BAND payment against the open invoice, so no money passes through the Stripe balance and it can never appear in a payout or on the bank line this report is compared against — the page links to R-166's deposit slips instead of blending the two into a number that reconciles to nothing. **The arithmetic is signed**: a payment taken in March and returned in April was genuinely in March's payouts and genuinely out of April's, so a settlement and a reversal are separately dated events off one row and a row can appear in both; netting only currently-`SETTLED` rows understates March and never shows April's clawback at all. Totals are **gross** — nothing in this product records a Stripe processing fee — and the window is settlement dates rather than payout dates, both named on the page (R-078's rule). **Found along the way:** this item's own reversal test asserted on the PORTFOLIO-wide total in a shared `rental_test`, went red once and green on retry under `fullyParallel`; the report was right the whole time and the assertion was unscoped. **Left behind:** nothing records the transfer itself, so "prove they did" still rests on the bank statement beside a report that is regenerated rather than archived (R-081d's split is the precedent, nothing owns it); no processing fee anywhere, so no net payout can ever be stated; `HAP_ACH` would be counted as a Stripe settlement if anything ever wrote it, and nothing does. Review finding 12 | PROP-02, RPT-05, RPT-07; D-11, D-191; review §12 | M | R-081a, R-166 | Arc 3 |
| 168 | ✅ R-181 | **A tenant who reports a problem hears something back.** Zero of the 40 notification templates acknowledged a maintenance request; SMS and phone-logged intake — the channels built specifically for the non-digital tenant — produced nothing the tenant could see before a vendor was dispatched, which may be days later. `ticket.acknowledged` now goes out on `ticket.created`, from ONE outbox consumer rather than from the five intake sites, so the sixth intake path somebody adds gets it for free (the same call R-023's triage consumer made about the same event). **Its own category, `maintenance_ack`, and that is the load-bearing decision** (D-192): `maintenance_update` defaults OFF on SMS, so folding the receipt into it would have shipped this item inert for exactly the tenant it exists for — the same fork R-058, R-059, R-064 and R-177 each resolved the same way. Separate from `maintenance_clarify` too, because muting a checklist you are asked to run must not mute the receipt for something you reported. **A texted-in request is deliberately skipped**, since R-177's `inviteToClarify` already answered it in seconds on the channel it arrived on — decided on `Ticket.source`, NOT on whether a clarify notification row exists, because `notify()` writes a row per channel even when it suppresses, so "a clarify row exists" is also true of a tenant who muted that category. The reference is `ticketReference()`, the cuid's own six-character tail uppercased (the scheme `confidential/actions.ts` and `locks/tenant-codes.ts` already use), printed on the staff and portal ticket screens as well — a handle the person taking the call cannot see is not a reference. **The row's second half turned out to be already built and was NOT duplicated**: `scheduleEntry` is the only writer of a SCHEDULED work order and already sends `entry.notice` to the reporting tenant on a locked category, so a `ticket.scheduled` template would have said the same facts to the same person in the same second; `e2e/entry-notice.spec.ts` now asserts that send so the claim is a test rather than a code read. **Found along the way:** this item's own test teardown hit the append-only trap head-on — deleting an `OutboxEvent` a notification points at fires a SET NULL UPDATE that the `Notification` trigger refuses, killing the whole delete. **Left behind:** a texted-in tenant never receives the quotable reference, and receives nothing at all in the rare case where clarify was correctly silent (no SMS consent, or a mint failure); the acknowledgement rides the hourly outbox cron, so it can lag the request by up to an hour; `entry.notice` still names no ticket, so a tenant with two open requests cannot tell which one a scheduled visit is for. | 169 | ✅ R-182 | **`JurisdictionRule` gets a day-count basis.** Every statutory clock in the product runs on `addBusinessDays`, which despite its name adds plain calendar days with no weekend or holiday roll — fine while Texas is the only configured state, wrong the moment a business-day-counting or roll-forward state is added, which is exactly what R-162's clone flow now makes easy to do without noticing the gap. Fix: `dayCountBasis` (calendar/business) and `rollForwardOnNonBusinessDay` fields per rule, a referenced holiday list, one shared deadline function every clock calls. **OQ-gated**: values per state need counsel review before that state's rule goes effective — covered by the existing pre-activation legal-review gate (flagged gaps §6), not a new process. ## How to use this backlog with Claude Code

- **Work strictly top to bottom.** The order encodes the dependency graph; when you reach an item, everything it depends on is already built. Don't cherry-pick a later item unless its entire "Depends on" chain is done.
- **One item (or one small cluster) per session.** S/M items are a single focused session. L items should be split into 2–3 sessions at the start ("build the state machine," then "build the UI on it"). R-097 (XL) must be split before starting.
- **Load the source PRD section as context.** The PRD/Feature column points at the authoritative story in `00-master-prd.md` §5. That story's Given/When/Then criteria are the item's definition of done — not this row's prose, which is the work order.
- **Start each session with:** the backlog row, the PRD story, `07-decisions.md`, the cross-cutting requirements in PRD §6 (auth, WCAG 2.1 AA, mobile-first, PCI scope, jurisdiction config), and the current schema. End each session with tests passing — **money math, jurisdiction-rule resolution, and the maintenance funnel are the three places tests are non-negotiable** — and the seed script updated if entities changed.
- **Check the gate before starting.** If the item's row or `07-decisions.md` names an unanswered OQ, either get the answer or build to the PRD's stated default and record the assumption in `PROGRESS.md`. Don't decide it silently.
- **Four demo checkpoints, one per milestone that closed** (D-28), each a kept spec rather than a manual pass — a walk nobody can repeat is a walk that happened once. After R-032, Golden Path 1 (leak text → vendor magic link → closed with photo → cost on the property). After R-050, Golden Path 2 (rent posts → autopay collects → late fee → reminder → magic-link payment → owner dashboard). After Milestone 8, Golden Path 3, the confidential path — `e2e/golden-path-3.spec.ts`. After Milestone 9, Golden Path 4, the stranger's path — `e2e/golden-path-4.spec.ts`. **Define the next one before the milestone's items, not after**: both 3 and 4 were owed by a milestone that had already closed.
| 171 | ✅ R-184 | **Demo checkpoint walk (D-28), Milestone 10.** A milestone closed and the last walk was R-105, which found seven defects across 88 routes that all returned 200. 250 staff routes, the tenant portal, and every public token surface (apply / prescreen / showing / sign / pay / vendor / vendor-bid / verify) walked in a browser at desktop and 412px phone widths. **Three real defects found and fixed.** (1) `requireScope` reported every denial as `no_permission`, so the OWNER of the portfolio opening `/money/deposits` was told *"Your role doesn't include this. Ask whoever manages access"* — they hold `ledger.adjust`; what they lacked was a proved second factor, and the person to ask was themselves. `requirePermission` had always made that distinction and sends people to enrolment; the scope-shaped guard 100 lines below it did not (D-196). (2) `/money`'s billing-runs rows hand-rolled `.toLowerCase().replace('_', '-')`, and `String.replace` with a string pattern replaces only the FIRST match — `MONTH_TO_MONTH` rendered `month-to_month` and `PENDING_SIGNATURE` rendered `pending-signature` instead of `awaiting signature`; `leaseStatusLabel` already existed and is what `/leases` uses. (3) A prospect's email address had no line-break opportunity, so it painted past its grid track and made `/prospects/[id]` **424px wide inside a 412px phone** — the D-194 reflow class again, one container up. Recorded and not fixed: the merge-field catalogue still renders raw `YYYY-MM-DD` into a real tenant email, and the demo seed leaves signature envelopes with no readable document | D-28; ROLE-05; D-194 | M | R-105, R-166 | Arc 3 |
| 172 | ✅ R-185 | **A merge field that is a date reads as a date, in all three catalogues.** R-184's walk found the template preview rendering *"rent … is due on 2026-10-01"* and the subject *"Rent for Bluebonnet Lane House is due 2026-10-01"* — a machine identifier posted to a person, by email and SMS, into the append-only `Message` trail. D-153's class, named first by R-179. **The defect was in three places, not one**: `comms/merge-fields.ts`, `leases/generation.ts` and `documents/template.ts` are separate catalogues sharing one `renderTemplate`, and each of their value builders wrote a raw `BusinessDate` — eight values across `lease.starts_on`, `lease.ends_on`, `balance.due_on`, `term.starts_on`, `term.ends_on` and three separate `today`s. The lease and document cases were the sharpest: `leaseDocumentBlocks` and `documentTemplateBlocks` already format `generatedOn` themselves, so one generated document carried *"Date prepared: 18 Aug 2026"* in its meta line and *"on 2026-08-18"* in the body a PM had merged. Every catalogue's `example` was ISO too, so the editor was telling the author the wrong thing as well (D-198) | D-153; COMM-03; DOC-04; LEASE-06 | S | R-116, R-184 | Arc 3 |
| 173 | ✅ R-186 | **The demo seed's two walk-only defects.** Both were recorded by R-184's Milestone 10 walk and neither is reachable from a test. (1) Every seeded `LeaseEnvelope` had `draftDocumentId` null, so the demo's live LEASE_SIGN link showed a **guarantor a signature form with nothing to read** — `/sign/[token]` renders its "Read the lease before signing" link behind `{link.documentId && …}`. Production cannot reach that state, so the fix is a seed that writes the real artifact: `leaseDocumentBlocks` and `renderBlocksPdf`, the product's own builder and renderer, with only the thirteen merge values assembled locally and a test tying them to `seed-lease-templates.mts`'s own `LEASE_BODY` (D-200). The walk also found the signature block reading **"Tenant 0"** — `orderedSigners` is one-based and the seed's loop was not. (2) `--reset` re-renamed rows it had already renamed, because `reset()` retires properties before legal entities and finds everything by entity name: a run that dies in between leaves the entity findable, so the next run retires the same properties again. `rental_demo` held eleven generations of `Bluebonnet Lane House (retired …) (retired …) (retired …)` and two `LegalEntity` rows sharing a name. One `retiredName` helper is now the single writer for all five renames, and the stamp reads `9 Sept 2026 13:39` rather than `2026-09-09T16:51` — those names are on `/vendors`, `/properties` and `/inspections/templates`, which list inactive rows deliberately (D-199, D-153's class) | D-28; D-153; LEASE-06 | S | R-184, R-185 | Arc 3 |

## Milestone 14: Arc 4 — the record holds up when somebody argues with it

Sourced from the 2026-09-09 operator review (`docs/reviews/2026-09-09-operator-review.md`), commissioned by D-201 after Arc 3 closed at R-186. Ordering: the six findings where the product's behaviour is **wrong** come first, then the rest by operational pain × frequency, matching the review's own ranking. Two rows are **Needs counsel** and say so; one more carries an unverified production frequency recorded as **unknown**. The review's **"Do not build" list is part of this milestone** and repeats three arcs of refusals: no accrual/interest engine before a second state is onboarded, no Stripe Connect, no settings screen for `CHASE_LADDER_DAYS` / `TURN_STAGE_DAYS` / `TURN_STALL_DAYS` / `PLAN_GRACE_DAYS` / the stall thresholds, no second queue for job failures or refunds or chase rungs (D-9, paid for three times), no backfill of anything — including the plans row 174 completed wrongly — and no per-stage turn table or second "days vacant" definition. Each row's claims are inherited evidence: **re-verify against the code before building** (R-150's lesson, and the reason two of these were spot-checked before the row was written).

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 174 | ✅ R-187 | **WRONG — a repayment plan counts ordinary rent as instalment money, so it can never break and eventually completes itself.** `paidTowardPlan` (`apps/web/lib/payments/plans.ts:50-67`) sums every `PAYMENT`/`CREDIT` on the lease since `startedOn` and nothing subtracts the rent charged in between — D-181's "the charges cancel on both sides" drops a term, and the correct predicate is `paymentsSince − chargesSince >= cumulativeMatured`. A $2,400 plan against a tenant paying only their ordinary $1,500 rent returns `ACTIVE` with `shortfallCents: 0` and **`COMPLETED`** six months later, while `payment_plan`'s `halt_dunning` + `halt_late_fees` keep the ladder and the late-fee meter off for the whole run. Net the charges (or take the balance), and drop `CREDIT` for D-181's own stated reason — a concession is us. **Do not backfill** the plans already completed wrongly; report them. Review finding 1 | PRD PAY-08/PAY-12; R-175; D-181; review §1 | M | R-175 | Arc 4 |
| 175 | ✅ R-188 | **WRONG — once the disposition letter is sent, nothing watches the refund deadline.** `deposit-disposition-reminder-job.ts:27` and `reports/queries.ts:239` both filter `dispositionSentAt: null`, so the halfway nudge, the overdue alarm and the `/reports` calendar all go silent at the moment the letter is generated — while Texas §92.103 runs refund and itemization on one clock. The only surface left, the `deposit_refund_due` Task, is dated the day it was raised (`deposits/actions.ts:315`) rather than `dispositionDueOn`, so it reads Overdue from day two and never names the statutory date. Key both readers on `refundPaidOn IS NULL AND refundedCents > 0` as well, and date the Task with the deadline. **Needs counsel**: whether a timely itemization with a late refund is a partial defence. Review finding 2. **Built as specified** (D-203): both readers take `OR: [{ dispositionSentAt: null }, { refundPaidOn: null, refundedCents: { gt: 0 } }]`, the task and the calendar row say *Deposit refund* once the letter is out and *Deposit disposition* before it, and `deposit_refund_due` carries `dispositionDueOn` as its business date and names it in the title. **No new task type and no new `CriticalDateKind`** — one statutory clock, one row, a label that says which half is outstanding (D-9). The counsel question turned out not to gate the build: the review's own *"either way the operator should be able to see the date"* is the whole of what the code does, and the answer changes no branch. **Found along the way:** `deposit-disposition.spec.ts`'s deposit fixture carried no `dispositionDueOn` at all, so the new Task silently took the fallback date — the fixture now seeds what `startDepositDisposition` stamps at move-out. Every other `dispositionSentAt` reader (rent roll, tax packet, handoff file, `computeDisposition`) is liability accounting and already keyed on `refundPaidOn`; the two deadline surfaces were the only two wrong. **Left behind:** the reminder job's already-flagged guard keys on `leaseId`, so a lease holding two deposits (SECURITY + PET) flags once for both — pre-existing, not touched | PRD INSP-05/PAY-11; R-169/R-170; review §2 | S | R-170 | Arc 4 |
| 176 | ✅ R-189 | **WRONG — the photograph a tenant texts at 11pm is discarded at the door.** `app/api/sms/inbound/route.ts:75-77` reads only `From`/`Body`/`MessageSid`; `NumMedia` and `MediaUrl0…N` appear nowhere in the repo, while the email path has stored and re-parented the same photo onto the ticket since R-097d (`inbound-attachments.ts`, `email-intake.ts:146-153`). On the channel R-021 measured as roughly half of all intake, the vendor is dispatched blind, the append-only `Message` trail is missing content the tenant demonstrably sent, and R-181 now texts back *"we have your request"* while the evidence is gone. Fetch with the Twilio credentials R-104 already wired (same provider, not a new relationship — not R-093), hand the bytes to `storeInboundAttachments`, re-parent exactly as the email path does, drop-and-count for an unrouted message. Review finding 3 | PRD MAINT-01/COMM-01/COMM-08; R-021/R-097d/R-177/R-181; review §3 | M | R-181 | Arc 4 |
| 177 | ✅ R-190 | **WRONG — a caught-up job runs a missed business date with today's clock and is then recorded SUCCEEDED for that date.** `jobs/runner.ts:252` passes the real `now` alongside a historical `businessDate`; six of the twenty-two jobs read the clock instead of the parameter (`ledger.late_fees`, `billing.sweep`, `billing.due_notices`, `billing.card_expiry`, `billing.predebit`, `payments.chase`). `chaseRungDue` matches days-past-grace exactly, so a cron gap over the day a tenancy hit a rung loses that rung for ever, while `createTask`'s `(type, subjectId, businessDate)` key mints one duplicate Task per catch-up date — and R-174's panel, built precisely so a legal clock cannot stop unnoticed, reports the lost day as done. Late fees survive by luck (`postLateFeeDelta` is delta-keyed), which is why nobody noticed. Pass an instant consistent with the date being replayed; a job that genuinely cannot be re-dated must say so rather than claim the day. Review finding 4 | PRD §6.5/§6.6; R-174; D-180; review §4 | S–M | R-174 | Arc 4 |
| 178 | ✅ R-191 | **WRONG — the stall sweep flags a case once and then never again, including the FHA response clock.** `alreadyFlagged` (`apps/web/lib/cases/case-stall-job.ts:32-35`) is `findFirst({ where: { type, subjectId } })` with **no status filter**, so a completed or canceled Task still matches and the condition can never raise a second one. R-158's defence of the missing `businessDate` is right and stays; the missing status filter is not. Sharpest case is the file's first: `accommodation.response_overdue` is EMERGENCY because D-89 says an unanswered request reads as denied, so a Task marked done without deciding the request silences the escalation for the life of that request. Same shape for abandonment, violations, evictions, disputes and R-178's `turnover.stalled`. Exclude `DONE`/`CANCELED` and add a cool-off (last flag's business date + N), keeping R-158's "one Task, not thirty". Review finding 5 **Built as specified, and wider than the row** (D-206): the check is now one shared `alreadyFlagged(type, subjectId, today)` in `apps/web/lib/tasks/already-flagged.ts` — an OPEN/IN_PROGRESS/BLOCKED Task suppresses at any age, a DONE/CANCELED one only until its own business date + `TASK_REFLAG_COOL_OFF_DAYS` (7). **The same unfiltered guard was hand-written in six job files, not one**, and all six now call it: this file, `court-date-reminder-job.ts`, `compliance/alert-job.ts`, `renewal-window-job.ts`, `renter-insurance-job.ts` and `deposit-disposition-reminder-job.ts`. The worst sibling was `compliance/alert-job.ts`: completing a RECURRING item advances `dueOn` a year, so next year's inspection was flagged never. **R-188's leftover is fixed with it** — the deposit reminder now subjects its Task on the deposit, so a lease holding SECURITY and PET deposits flags both — and its two titles printed a raw `YYYY-MM-DD` (D-153), now rendered | PRD RISK-13; D-89; R-158/R-178; review §5 | S | R-178 | Arc 4 |
| 179 | ✅ R-192 | **WRONG — an online invoice payment can claim an unclaimed counter-payment row, so the second payment exists nowhere.** D-177's claim branch (`billing/webhook.ts:535-552`) runs when `stripePaymentIntentId == null && stripeInvoiceId != null` — and `events.ts:386` records that the account's API version (`2026-07-29.dahlia`) dropped `payment_intent` from the invoice object, so that is **every** invoice-driven payment, card and ACH included. A $750 counter cheque whose own `invoice.updated` never arrived sits unclaimed, the next $750 online payment on that invoice lands on it and returns it unchanged, and one ledger entry is written for two payments: the tenant is credited once, the balance stays $750 high, and the `Payment` row says *cheque, received at the counter* for money that came off a card. Bound the claim to rows received within a day or two, and count unclaimed-older-than-N offline rows on `/money`'s existing drift surface. Production frequency depends on real Stripe redelivery and is recorded **unknown** — the code path is verified, the frequency is not. Review finding 6 | PRD PAY-01/PAY-05; R-171; D-177; review §6 | S | R-171 | Arc 4 |

Property tax and the landlord policy are commonly a third of gross rent on a $1,500/month house, so every per-property Net is overstated and the lemon test ranks houses on a cost base that omits the largest thing that differs between them. A `PropertyExpense` row (property or entity, `bookedOn`, `ScheduleEKey`, cents, optional document, optional monthly recurrence) feeding the same export pipeline. **Not a ledger write** — D-11 governs the tenant-receivable projection, and vendor invoices already establish that owner-side outlay lives in its own table. **Built, and the premise was half wrong (D-208):** D-76 already let a vendor-invoice split carry all three lines. The table was built anyway at the owner's choice, for monthly recurrence and to keep a county out of the vendor list. The e-sign machinery (R-063/R-090), the document generation (R-062) and the portal's per-lease panels all already exist. This one crawled **236 staff routes** as `owner@demo.test` against the local `rental_demo`, all 236 again at a 412px phone, the **tenant portal through five tenants' magic links** and the vendor job link, and probed what each page SAYS rather than what it returns. **Every page returned 200 and nothing overflowed the phone** — R-184's D-197 discipline holds. What it found was one class on six surfaces and one on two, plus two seed defects. **D-153's sixth instance, sharpest on the eviction packet**: `Cure period runs from:` and `Last day to cure:` printed `2026-09-09` on the PDF an attorney files from, while `asDate` formats all seven of that packet's other dates and both screens showing the same two values already call `friendlyBusinessDate` — which is why no test and no screen could see it.
